<result>## Verdict up front

**No. `streamer.share` does NOT do production remote-UI loading.** The user's belief is incorrect.

The packaged Electron app loads its renderer from a **local file inside the asar** (`loadFile`). There is no remote UI bundle, no OTA UI mechanism, no `app://` protocol handler, no manifest fetch, no downloaded-and-extracted bundle. Any UI change requires a full rebuild + re-release of the Electron app.

---

## 1. Is there an Electron app? Yes

`C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\streamer.share\apps\desktop` — `electron-vite` + `electron-builder`.

- `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\streamer.share\apps\desktop\package.json` → `"main": "out/main/index.js"`, and the whole `electron-builder` config lives inline in its `"build"` block (`appId: live.screenstream`, `asar: true`, `files: ["out/**","package.json"]`).
- Main process: `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\streamer.share\apps\desktop\src\main\index.ts` (1611 lines)
- Preload: `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\streamer.share\apps\desktop\src\preload\index.ts`
- Renderer: `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\streamer.share\apps\desktop\src\renderer\`
- Build config: `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\streamer.share\apps\desktop\electron.vite.config.ts`

No forge. No separate `electron-builder.yml`.

---

## 2. How the renderer loads in production — LOCAL FILE

`apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts:72`:

```ts
const isDev = !app.isPackaged;
```

`apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1477-1489` — the entire URL decision:

```ts
  if (isDev) {
    const { loaded } = await tryLoadDevRenderer(mainWindow);
    if (!loaded &amp;&amp; !mainWindow.isDestroyed()) {
      await loadDevRendererFailurePage(mainWindow);
    }
  } else {
    const indexPath = path.join(mainDir, '../renderer/index.html');
    try {
      await mainWindow.loadFile(indexPath);
    } catch (error) {
      console.warn('Failed to load renderer index', error);
    }
  }
```

That is the **only** production load path. `mainDir` is `path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))` (line 63-64), so in a packaged app it resolves to `app.asar/out/renderer/index.html`.

**Verified against the actual compiled build**, not just source — `apps/desktop/out/main/index.js` contains `loadFile(indexPath)`. There is no env var, feature flag, or config anywhere that can redirect the production renderer to a remote URL.

Every `loadURL`/`loadFile` in the whole repo (excluding `node_modules`):

| File:line                | What it loads                                                 | When           |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- |
| `src/main/index.ts:894`  | `window.loadURL(candidate)` — localhost dev server candidates | **dev only**   |
| `src/main/index.ts:930`  | `data:text/html,...` dev failure page                         | **dev only**   |
| `src/main/index.ts:1485` | `loadFile(indexPath)` — local `index.html`                    | **production** |
| `src/main/auth.ts:169`   | `authWindow.loadURL(authUrl)` — OAuth popup (see below)       | both           |

## 3. OTA / remote-bundle mechanism: none

Exhaustive greps across the repo returned **zero** hits for:

- `protocol.registerFileProtocol`, `registerSchemesAsPrivileged`, `registerBufferProtocol`, `protocol.handle`
- `app://` custom scheme
- `unzip` / `extract-zip` / `adm-zip` / `.tar` / runtime `.asar` swapping
- `webContents.reload()`, `reloadIgnoringCache`, cache-busting
- service worker registration in the desktop renderer
- any version-manifest JSON fetch for UI

The **only** `app.getPath('userData')` use in the entire codebase is `apps/desktop/src/main/authSession.ts:60`:

```ts
return path.join(app.getPath('userData'), 'session.enc');
```

— an encrypted auth token via `safeStorage`, not a UI bundle.

### electron-updater is present — but it's FULL-APP updates, not UI-only

`apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts:213-214, 1297-1348`:

```ts
const shouldCheckForUpdates = !isDev &amp;&amp; process.env.DISABLE_AUTO_UPDATE !== 'true';
const { autoUpdater } = updater;
...
autoUpdater.autoDownload = true;
autoUpdater.autoInstallOnAppQuit = true;
autoUpdater.on('update-downloaded', async () =&gt; { /* dialog: "Restart now" */ });
```

with `UPDATE_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS = 4 * 60 * 60 * 1000` (line 36), and install via `autoUpdater.quitAndInstall(false, true)` (line 978).

Feed comes from `apps/desktop/package.json` `build.publish`:

```json
"publish": [{ "provider": "generic", "url": "https://downloads.screenstream.live" }]
```

This downloads a **whole new signed installer** (`.dmg` / `.exe`) and replaces the app on restart. `apps/desktop/scripts/upload-r2.mjs` only ever uploads `.dmg`, `.exe`, `.zip`, `.blockmap`, `latest.yml` / `latest-mac.yml`, plus `latest/` aliases — no HTML/JS bundles. Documented in `docs/cdn-releases.md` and `docs/desktop-release.md`.

**This is probably the source of the misconception**: there IS a CDN, there IS an auto-updating pipeline, and updates land silently within 4 hours — but it's a full app replacement requiring a signed rebuild, not hot UI swapping.

## 4. Remote URLs the desktop _does_ touch (none are the app UI)

1. **OAuth popup** — `apps/desktop/src/main/auth.ts:158-169`:

```ts
const authWindow = new BrowserWindow({
  width: 500,
  height: 700,
  show: true,
  webPreferences: { nodeIntegration: false, contextIsolation: true },
});
authWindow.loadURL(authUrl);
```

`authUrl` = `createSiteUrl('/api/auth/signin/social', { provider })` → `https://decisive-wolverine-16.convex.site/...`. **No `preload` is attached**, so this remote page has zero IPC bridge access. It's closed as soon as a token is captured via `will-redirect` / `did-navigate`.

2. **`shell.openExternal`** for the web portal — `index.ts:1293` (`openWebPortal`) and `index.ts:793` (`attemptWebSessionImport`). These open the user's **system browser**, not an in-app window.

3. Convex HTTP/WebSocket API calls from `authSession.ts` / `authApi.ts`.

### webPreferences / security posture

`apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1428-1433`:

```ts
webPreferences: {
  contextIsolation: true,
  nodeIntegration: false,
  preload: path.join(mainDir, '../preload/index.mjs'),
  sandbox: false,
},
```

Notably **absent** across the repo — and this is itself strong evidence remote loading was never designed:

- No `will-navigate` handler
- No `setWindowOpenHandler`
- No origin allowlist
- No CSP (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/index.html` has no CSP meta tag, and no `onHeadersReceived` CSP injection exists)
- No `webSecurity` / `allowRunningInsecureContent` toggles
- No `&lt;webview&gt;`, `BrowserView`, or `WebContentsView` anywhere

The preload (`src/preload/index.ts:113-116`) exposes a large privileged surface (`saveRecording`, `getDesktopCaptureSources`, `getLiveKitToken`, `signIn`…) via `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('app', appAPI)`. That is safe **only** because the renderer is trusted local `file://`. Pointing this at a remote origin as-is would be a serious security hole.

### Offline / first-run

Not applicable — the UI is always local, so there is nothing to fall back to. The only "failure page" (`DEV_RENDERER_FAILURE_HTML`, line 44-45) is dev-only.

## 5. Hosting

- **Web app** (`apps/web`, a separate Vite/React SPA — landing, pricing, download, `/portal/watch`, `/portal/start`, `/portal/account`) → **Vercel**. Root `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\streamer.share\vercel.json`:

```json
{
  "buildCommand": "bash scripts/vercel-build.sh",
  "outputDirectory": "apps/web/dist",
  "rewrites": [{ "source": "/(.*)", "destination": "/index.html" }]
}
```

Production URL: `https://screenstream.live` (per `apps/desktop/production-public-env.ts` → `VITE_WEB_PORTAL_URL`).

- **Desktop installers + update feeds** → **Cloudflare R2** behind `https://downloads.screenstream.live` (`docs/cdn-releases.md`, `apps/desktop/scripts/upload-r2.mjs`, `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml`).

- **Backend** → Convex (`https://decisive-wolverine-16.convex.cloud` / `.convex.site`), inlined at build time by `apps/desktop/production-public-env.ts` + `electron.vite.config.ts` `define`.

Important: `apps/web` and `apps/desktop/src/renderer` are **two distinct React apps**. They share `packages/auth-ui` and `packages/shared-types`, but as workspace **source** dependencies compiled into each bundle separately — nothing is fetched at runtime. The Vercel-hosted app is the _viewer portal_; the Electron renderer is the _host control room_.

---

## 6. Dev hot reload (exists) vs production remote UI (does not)

**DEV — real, and fairly elaborate:**

- `electron-vite dev` (`apps/desktop/package.json` → `"dev": "electron-vite dev"`), which runs a Vite dev server with standard HMR for the renderer.
- `getDevServerCandidates()` (`index.ts:139-163`) reads `ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL` / `VITE_DEV_SERVER_URL`, defaults to port 5173, and **port-scans ±5** on `localhost` and `127.0.0.1`.
- `tryLoadDevRenderer()` (`index.ts:868-926`) retries 24× at 500 ms, waiting for an `app:renderer-ready` IPC signal per attempt.
- `attemptDevRendererRecovery()` (`index.ts:1437-1475`) re-loads on `did-fail-load` / `render-process-gone`, with a 1.5 s cooldown.
- `restartDevWindow()` (`index.ts:935-945`) — in dev, "Restart" re-loads the dev URL instead of relaunching the process.

**Every one of these is gated on `if (isDev)` / `isDev` (i.e. `!app.isPackaged`) and is dead code in a packaged build.**

No `electron-reload` or `electron-reloader` package is installed (checked `bun.lock` and both `package.json`s).

---

## Bonus: where the idea may have come from

The sibling reference repo `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\t3code-release-ref` (explicitly cited in `streamer.share/docs/desktop-release.md:87` as "the t3code reference") _does_ use `loadURL` in production — `apps/desktop/src/window/DesktopWindow.ts:307-312`:

```ts
if (environment.isDevelopment) {
  void window.loadURL(applicationUrl);
  window.webContents.openDevTools({ mode: 'detach' });
} else {
  void window.loadURL(applicationUrl);
}
```

But `applicationUrl` is `backendConfig.httpBaseUrl` (line 321-323) — a **locally spawned backend HTTP server** at `http://127.0.0.1:5733/` (confirmed in `DesktopWindow.test.ts:203`). That's still shipped-locally UI served over loopback, **not** a remote CDN. So neither repo does production remote-UI loading.

---

## Summary of what would need to be built (nothing exists today)

To get production remote UI in `streamer.share` you would be starting from zero on: a remote/hosted renderer target, an origin allowlist + `will-navigate`/`setWindowOpenHandler` guards, a CSP, `sandbox: true` hardening, a locally bundled offline fallback, and a decision about how (or whether) to expose the `window.app` preload bridge to a remote origin. None of that scaffolding is present.</result>

## Assistant — 2026-07-30 17:54

**Verdict on streamer.share: it does not do what you remembered.** Its packaged Electron app loads the renderer from a local file inside the asar (`loadFile(path.join(mainDir, '../renderer/index.html'))` at `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1485`). There is no remote UI bundle, no `app://` protocol handler, no manifest fetch, no OTA extraction.

What streamer.share _does_ have — and what likely created the impression — is `electron-updater` with a generic feed at `https://downloads.screenstream.live` (Cloudflare R2), checking every 4 hours and auto-installing on restart. That's a **full signed app replacement**, not a UI-only swap. A CSS tweak still requires a full rebuild + re-sign + re-release.

Worth noting for the plan: streamer.share also has **no** `will-navigate` guard, no `setWindowOpenHandler`, no CSP, and `sandbox: false` with a large privileged `contextBridge` surface — i.e. none of the hardening remote-UI loading would require. So there's no prior art to copy from there; we'd design this fresh.

Still waiting on the Agendex exploration before I design the approach.

# 1. Repo layout &amp; tooling

**Bun workspaces monorepo** (no turbo/nx/pnpm). Root `C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\Agendex\package.json` declares `"workspaces": ["packages/*"]`, lockfile is `bun.lock`. Lint/format via `oxlint`/`oxfmt`. Versioning via `@changesets/cli` (`.changeset/config.json` — note `"ignore": ["@agendex/desktop"]`).

| Package                                 | Role                                                                                                                          |
| --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `packages/desktop` (`@agendex/desktop`) | **Electron main + preload only.** No renderer of its own.                                                                     |
| `packages/ee` (`@agendex/ee`)           | **The renderer/UI actually shipped in the desktop app.** Vite + React 19 + wouter + Convex. Also the Vercel-deployed web app. |
| `packages/app` (`@agendex/app`)         | OSS local app: the **Hono API server** (exported as `@agendex/app/server`) + an OSS Vite client.                              |
| `packages/web` (`@agendex/web`)         | Shared React UI component library (source-only, consumed by `ee` and `app`). Holds `DownloadPage.tsx`.                        |
| `packages/shared`                       | Domain logic, adapters, config, convex-url helpers.                                                                           |
| `packages/cli` (`agendex-cli`, v4.2.1)  | Published npm CLI + daemon runtime (aliased into the Electron main bundle).                                                   |

Root scripts of interest (`package.json:14-22`):

```
"desktop:dev":  "bun run --cwd ./packages/desktop dev",
"desktop:build":"bun run --cwd ./packages/desktop build",
"desktop:dist": "bun run desktop:build &amp;&amp; bun run --cwd ./packages/desktop dist",
"release:desktop:prepare": "node ./scripts/prepare-desktop-release.mjs",
"build:cloud":  "cd packages/ee &amp;&amp; bunx convex deploy --cmd 'bunx --bun vite build'",
```

`packages/desktop/package.json`:

```json
"main": "./out/main/index.js",
"version": "0.0.0",
"dev":   "concurrently ... \"bun run --cwd ../ee dev:client\" \"cross-env AGENDEX_RENDERER_URL=http://app.agendex.localhost:5174/dashboard electron-vite dev --watch\"",
"build": "bun run --cwd ../ee build &amp;&amp; electron-vite build",
"dist":  "electron-builder --publish never"
```

---

# 2. Electron main process — **the single most important finding**

**In production the app does NOT use `file://` or `loadFile`.** It boots an in-process Hono HTTP server on an ephemeral `localhost` port that serves the SPA **and** the local API **and** a WebSocket from one origin, then `loadURL`s it.

`C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\Agendex\packages\desktop\src\main\index.ts:65-110`:

```ts
function resolveClientDistDir(): string {
  if (app.isPackaged) {
    // electron-builder copies the built EE client to resources/client (see electron-builder.yml).
    return join(process.resourcesPath, 'client');
  }
  // Dev/unpackaged prod-serve: out/main -&gt; packages/ee/dist
  return join(__dirname, '../../../ee/dist');
}

async function ensureBackend() {
  ...
  const clientDistDir = resolveClientDistDir();
  if (is.dev) {
    server = await startNodeServer({ port: DEV_SERVER_PORT /* 4890 */, clientDistDir });
    localApiToken = server.token;
    rendererTargetUrl = process.env.AGENDEX_RENDERER_URL ?? DEV_RENDERER_URL;
  } else {
    // Prod: ephemeral port on `localhost` (a cloud-trusted origin so better-auth
    // CORS accepts the desktop), client + local API served from the same origin.
    server = await startNodeServer({ port: 0, clientDistDir, hostname: 'localhost' });
    localApiToken = server.token;
    rendererTargetUrl = `http://localhost:${server.port}/dashboard`;
  }
}
```

Constants at `index.ts:46-47`: `DEV_SERVER_PORT = 4890`, `DEV_RENDERER_URL = 'http://app.agendex.localhost:5174/dashboard'`.

**Dev/prod switch** is `is.dev` from `@electron-toolkit/utils` (i.e. `!app.isPackaged`). There is **no** `MAIN_VITE_DEV_SERVER_URL` and **no** `NODE_ENV` branching anywhere in `packages/desktop/src`. `AGENDEX_RENDERER_URL` exists but is **only honored when `is.dev`** — a natural extension point.

**BrowserWindow creation** — `packages\desktop\src\main\desktop-window.ts:10-58`:

```ts
export function createDesktopWindow(targetUrl: string, onClosed: () =&gt; void): BrowserWindow {
  const window = new BrowserWindow({
    width: 1280, height: 832, minWidth: 720, minHeight: 480,
    show: false, backgroundColor: '#041f1d',
    ...(isMac ? { frame: false } : {}),
    webPreferences: {
      preload: join(__dirname, '../preload/index.js'),
      sandbox: false,
      contextIsolation: true,
      nodeIntegration: false,
    },
  });
  window.webContents.setWindowOpenHandler(({ url }) =&gt; { if (shouldOpenWindowExternally(url)) void shell.openExternal(url); return { action: 'deny' }; });
  window.webContents.on('will-navigate', (event, url) =&gt; {
    if (!shouldOpenNavigationExternally(targetUrl, url)) return;
    event.preventDefault();
    void shell.openExternal(url);
  });
  ...
  loadWithRetry(window, targetUrl);
}
```

`packages\desktop\src\main\window-loader.ts` — retries `loadURL` 20× at 300 ms on `did-fail-load`. Useful precedent for a "download-then-load" flow.

`packages\desktop\src\main\desktop-navigation.ts` — **any http(s) navigation to a different origin than `targetUrl` is forced out to the system browser.** A remote-UI design that changes the window origin must account for this (`shouldOpenNavigationExternally`).

**CSP / session / protocol:**

- **No CSP anywhere.** No `session.defaultSession.webRequest.onHeadersReceived`, no `&lt;meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy"&gt;` in `packages/ee/index.html` or the built `dist/index.html`. (The page already loads Google Fonts cross-origin.)
- **No `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged`, no `protocol.handle`, no custom `app://`.** The only protocol work is the OAuth deep link: `packages\desktop\src\main\desktop-protocol.ts` (`agendex://auth/callback`) + `desktop-protocol-lifecycle.ts` (`app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient('agendex')`, `open-url`, `second-instance`) + `packages\desktop\build\installer.nsh` (Windows registry keys).
- No `setPermissionRequestHandler`.

**Preload** — `packages\desktop\src\preload\index.ts`. `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('agendexDesktop', {...})` exposing `cloudToken`, `convexSiteUrl`, `login/logout`, `authFetch`, `checkForUpdates`, `installUpdate`, `getUpdateState`, `getAppVersion`, `getBuildInfo`, zoom helpers. It also does a **synchronous** `ipcRenderer.sendSync('agendex:get-bootstrap')` and writes `localStorage.agendex_token` + `agendex_dashboard_mode` **before page scripts run** (because the origin's port changes every launch, so localStorage is effectively fresh each run).

**The static file server** — `packages\app\src\server\node-server.ts:93-118` (this is what you'd repoint at a remote/downloaded bundle):

```ts
function mountStaticFromDir(app: Hono, clientDistDir: string) {
  const indexPath = join(clientDistDir, 'index.html');
  app.get('/*', (c) =&gt; {
    const pathname = new URL(c.req.url).pathname;
    const file = resolveStaticFile(clientDistDir, pathname);
    if (file) return c.body(readFileSync(file), 200, { 'Content-Type': contentTypeFor(file) });
    if (looksLikeAssetPath(pathname)) return c.text('Not found', 404);   // no SPA fallback for real assets
    if (existsSync(indexPath)) return c.body(readFileSync(indexPath), 200, { 'Content-Type': 'text/html; charset=utf-8' });
    return c.text('Not found', 404);
  });
}
```

Route order is set in `packages\app\src\server\app.ts:104-125`: `/api/v1/ws` (token in query), `/api/v1/health`, `authMiddleware` on `/api/*`, `app.route('/api/v1', plans)`, then `mountStatic(app)` last as catch-all.

---

# 3. Build tooling

**`packages\desktop\electron.vite.config.ts`** — builds **only `main` and `preload`** (renderer is out-of-band). `externalizeDeps: false` so the TS workspace packages get bundled; `external: ['electron', 'bufferutil', 'utf-8-validate']`. Two main inputs: `src/main/index.ts` and `src/main/daemon-worker.ts`. Alias `@agendex/daemon-runtime` → `../cli/src/daemon-runtime.ts`.

**`packages\desktop\electron-builder.yml`** (full key parts):

```yaml
appId: dev.agendex.desktop
productName: Agendex
artifactName: Agendex-${version}-${arch}.${ext}
directories: { output: release, buildResources: build }

files:
  - out/**/*
  - package.json
  - '!node_modules/**/*'

# The built EE web client is served by the in-process Node server at runtime via
# process.resourcesPath/client (see src/main/index.ts resolveClientDistDir).
extraResources:
  - from: ../ee/dist
    to: client
    filter: ['**/*']

npmRebuild: false
asar: true

publish:
  provider: generic
  url: https://github.com/Tyru5/Agendex/releases/latest/download

mac:  { hardenedRuntime: true, gatekeeperAssess: false, protocols: [{name: Agendex Auth, schemes: [agendex]}], target: [dmg, zip] }
win:  { target: [ {target: nsis, arch:[x64]}, {target: portable, arch:[x64]} ] }
nsis: { oneClick: false, perMachine: false, allowToChangeInstallationDirectory: true, include: build/installer.nsh }
```

Key consequence: **the UI lives in `resources/client`, outside `app.asar`, as plain files.** No `asarUnpack` needed. `publish` is a _generic_ provider (not `github`) because release tags are `desktop-v*`, which electron-updater's github provider can't parse.

**Code signing:** macOS is required (`CSC_LINK`, `CSC_KEY_PASSWORD`, `APPLE_API_KEY*`/`APPLE_ID`), hardened runtime + notarize + staple in `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml`. Windows signing is **optional** (`WIN_CSC_LINK`/`WIN_CSC_KEY_PASSWORD`); currently unsigned. See `docs/code-signing.md`, `docs/desktop-release.md`.

**No electron-forge.** Only electron-builder + electron-vite.

---

# 4. The UI / renderer

- **Framework:** React 19 + Vite 7 + Tailwind 4, router = **wouter** (`Switch`/`Route`, real history paths, no hash router, no `base`).
- **Pure SPA, no SSR.** Entry `packages\ee\index.html` → `packages\ee\src\main.tsx` → `packages\ee\src\App.tsx` (~3000 lines).
- **Build:** `bun run --cwd ../ee build` → `bunx --bun vite build` → **`packages/ee/dist`** (hashed assets under `dist/assets`, ~8.7 MB, 251 chunks; `index-&lt;hash&gt;.js` 1.5 MB + `index-&lt;hash&gt;.css` 230 KB). `packages/ee/dist` is gitignored.
- Routes (`packages\ee\src\App.tsx:2993-3068`): `/auth/check`, `/auth/cli`, `/auth/desktop`, `/login`, `/signup`, `/shared/:token`, `/about-me`, `/changelog`, `/docs`, `/download`, `/tools`, `/welcome`, `/invite/:token`, `/settings`, `/dashboard`, `/`. Desktop always lands on `/dashboard`.
- **Assumes `http://` same-origin, never `file://`:**
  - `packages\web\src\client\lib\api.ts:1` — `const BASE = '/api/v1';` (relative)
  - `packages\web\src\client\hooks\useBackendStatus.ts:16` — `fetch('/api/v1/health')`
  - `packages\web\src\client\hooks\useSocket.ts:10-17` — `import.meta.env.DEV ? ws://${location.hostname}:4890 : ws://${location.host}` + `/api/v1/ws?token=…` from `localStorage.agendex_token`
  - Built `index.html` references `/assets/...` absolute paths → requires a server root, not `file://`.
- **Build-time baked env:** `VITE_CONVEX_URL`, `VITE_CONVEX_SITE_URL`, `VITE_APP_URL`, `VITE_MARKETING_URL` (`packages/ee/.env.local`, `envDir: '.'`). **But the desktop deliberately avoids depending on them:** `packages\ee\src\lib\convex-client.ts` prefers `getDesktopConvexCloudUrl()` (derived at runtime from the login site URL) and falls back to a `https://placeholder.convex.cloud`. Comment: _"the desktop build needs no baked Convex env."_ This makes a remotely-fetched bundle safe.
- Desktop detection: `packages\ee\src\lib\desktop.ts` → `window.agendexDesktop`. `main.tsx` sets `document.documentElement.dataset.agendexDesktop = 'true'` and **skips `@vercel/analytics`** on desktop (the SPA fallback would return HTML for `/_vercel/insights/script.js`).

---

# 5. Existing update / versioning machinery

- **`packages\desktop\src\main\desktop-updater.ts`** — a clean, dependency-injected `createDesktopUpdater({ updater, isPackaged, isPortable, promptToRestart, notifyUpToDate, onStateChange, log, checkIntervalMs, initialDelayMs })`. Defaults: `4 h` interval, `10 s` initial delay, `autoDownload = true`, `autoInstallOnAppQuit = true`. States: `idle | checking | downloading | ready | no-update | error | unsupported`. `isPortableWindowsBuild()` disables self-update when `PORTABLE_EXECUTABLE_FILE` is set. **This is the model to clone for a UI updater.**
- Wiring in `index.ts:190-230`: IPC `agendex:update:check`, `agendex:update:install`, `agendex:update:get-state`, `agendex:get-app-version` (`app.getVersion()`), `agendex:get-build-info`; state pushed to renderer via `mainWindow.webContents.send('agendex:update:state', state)`.
- Renderer side: `packages\ee\src\hooks\useDesktopUpdate.ts` (listens to the `agendex:update:state` CustomEvent re-dispatched by the preload) and `packages\ee\src\components\SettingsPage.tsx`.
- `packages\desktop\src\main\desktop-build-info.ts` — reads `resources/app-update.yml` and checks for `publisherName` to report code-signed status.
- **Feed URL:** `https://github.com/Tyru5/Agendex/releases/latest/download` (generic provider → `latest.yml` / `latest-mac.yml`).
- **No S3/CDN/Vercel-blob asset hosting, no "channel" concept, no feature flags, no remote asset loading of any kind.** I grepped `cdn|s3|cloudfront|bucket|channel|feature.?flag|remote.?ui|hot.?reload` across `packages/desktop`, `packages/shared`, `docs`, `README.md` — only unrelated hits (IPC "channel", Codex "rollout" files).

---

# 6. Backend / API

Two backends:

1. **Local, in-process** — Hono, started by the main process (`startNodeServer`), token-authed (`packages/app/src/server/auth.ts`, token injected into `localStorage.agendex_token` by the preload). Serves `/api/v1/*`, `/api/v1/ws`, and the SPA.
2. **Hosted cloud (Convex + better-auth)** — `packages\desktop\src\main\cloud-auth.ts:18-25`:

```ts
const PROD_SITE_URL = 'https://app.agendex.dev';
const DEV_SITE_URL = 'http://app.agendex.localhost:5174';
export function getSiteUrl(isDev: boolean): string {
  return process.env.AGENDEX_SITE_URL ?? (isDev ? DEV_SITE_URL : PROD_SITE_URL);
}
```

Convex deployment URL is derived from the site URL at runtime via `packages\shared\src\convex-url.ts` (`*.convex.site` → `*.convex.cloud`).

**Yes — a hosted deployment of the exact same UI already exists.** Root `vercel.json`:

```json
{
  "buildCommand": "cd packages/ee &amp;&amp; bunx convex deploy --cmd 'cd ../.. &amp;&amp; bun run build:cloud'",
  "outputDirectory": "packages/ee/dist",
  "installCommand": "bun install",
  "git": { "deploymentEnabled": { "main": true, "*": false } },
  "rewrites": [
    { "source": "/install.sh", "destination": "/install.sh" },
    { "source": "/install.ps1", "destination": "/install.ps1" },
    { "source": "/(.*)", "destination": "/index.html" }
  ]
}
```

So `agendex.dev` / `app.agendex.dev` already serves **byte-for-byte the same `packages/ee/dist`** the desktop ships in `resources/client` — a ready-made remote origin for UI bundles.

---

# 7. Where `desktop version 1.4.15` lives

**Exactly one place in the repo:**

`C:\Users\Tyrus\Documents\ti\Agendex\packages\web\src\client\components\DownloadPage.tsx:18`

```ts
const DESKTOP_VERSION = '1.4.15';
const DESKTOP_TAG = `desktop-v${DESKTOP_VERSION}`;
const GITHUB_RELEASES_URL = 'https://github.com/Tyru5/Agendex/releases';
```

Commit `483122e chore: bump desktop version to 1.4.15`.

`packages/desktop/package.json` stays at `"version": "0.0.0"` in-repo; `scripts\prepare-desktop-release.mjs --write` rewrites both that file (ephemerally, on CI runners) and the `DESKTOP_VERSION` constant (committed, stable releases only). Release preflight in `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:102-113` **fails the release** if the download page is stale. Tag format: `desktop-v1.4.15`.

---

# Implications for a remote-updatable UI (what the code already gives you)

1. **No `file://` migration needed.** The renderer is already served over `http://localhost:&lt;port&gt;` by a Node server you control. Changing `resolveClientDistDir()` (`packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:65-72`) from `process.resourcesPath/client` to a downloaded, versioned directory under `app.getPath('userData')` is essentially the whole mechanism — relative `/api/v1` fetches, the WS URL, preload bridge, and cookie/localStorage origin all stay identical.
2. **`resources/client` is not a viable write target.** On macOS it sits inside the signed/notarized `.app`; mutating it breaks the signature and it isn't user-writable. Use `app.getPath('userData')` and keep the shipped `resources/client` as the immutable fallback/floor.
3. **The port is ephemeral (`port: 0`) every launch**, so localStorage is effectively wiped per run (hence the preload bootstrap injection and the main-process `dashboard-mode.ts` pref file). If a remote UI wants durable per-origin storage, you'd need a stable port or continue routing state through IPC.
4. **`shouldOpenNavigationExternally`** (`desktop-navigation.ts`) will punt any cross-origin navigation to the system browser — so loading the UI _directly_ from `https://app.agendex.dev` would need that guard updated, plus the relative `/api/v1` calls would hit the wrong host. Serving downloaded bundles through the existing local server avoids both problems entirely.
5. **`looksLikeAssetPath` + the `MIME_TYPES` map** (`node-server.ts:24-86`) are the allowlist for served extensions; any new asset type in a remote bundle must be added there or it 404s.
6. **`createDesktopUpdater`** is already the right shape (injected deps, state machine, IPC + renderer hook) to clone as a `createUiUpdater` — and `UpdateStatus`/`useDesktopUpdate` already have UI plumbing you could reuse.
7. **No CSP exists**, so adding one is a greenfield decision rather than a migration — but it becomes load-bearing the moment you execute remotely-fetched JS.</result>

```ts
// packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:65-72
function resolveClientDistDir(): string {
  if (app.isPackaged) return join(process.resourcesPath, 'client');
  return join(__dirname, '../../../ee/dist');
}
```

The UI ships as **plain files in `resources/client`** (via `extraResources`, outside the asar). So the whole mechanism reduces to: _point that resolver at a downloaded, verified bundle in `userData`_. Origin stays identical, so the preload bridge, relative `/api/v1` fetches, the WS URL, `localStorage` bootstrap, and the `shouldOpenNavigationExternally` guard all keep working untouched.

Two things worth calling out from the research:

**streamer.share doesn't do this.** Its packaged app loads the renderer from a local file inside the asar. What it has is `electron-updater` against a Cloudflare R2 feed — a full signed app replacement, not a UI-only swap. There was no prior art to copy.

**Agendex is already most of the way there, though.** The packaged app never uses `file://` — it boots an in-process Hono server and serves the SPA over `http://localhost:<port>` from a plain directory (`resources/client`, shipped via `extraResources`, outside the asar). So this feature is largely _pointing that one resolver at a downloaded directory_. The origin never changes, which means the preload bridge, relative `/api/v1` calls, the WebSocket, the `localStorage` bootstrap, and the external-navigation guard all keep working untouched. That's a much smaller and lower-risk change than it would have been in streamer.share, where it would have meant building remote loading plus CSP, origin allowlisting, and sandbox hardening from zero.

The plan covers the bundle store under `userData`, Ed25519-signed manifests verified with Node's built-in `crypto` (no new dep for verification), a `minShellVersion` gate so UI and shell stay safely decoupled, and a boot sentinel that quarantines a bad bundle and reverts to the shipped floor. One bonus that fell out: Windows portable builds can't self-update the app, but `userData` is still writable — so they _can_ take UI updates.

---

# Remote-updatable UI for packaged Agendex desktop builds

## Context

Today any UI-only change to Agendex — a copy tweak, a spacing fix, a new settings toggle — requires a full desktop release: bump `DESKTOP_VERSION`, tag `desktop-v*`, run the ~2h `desktop-release.yml` (macOS notarization alone can stall for an hour), and wait for every user's `electron-updater` cycle to pull a fresh signed installer. That is a very expensive pipeline for changes that never touch the Electron shell.

The goal is to ship UI changes independently of the shell: publish a new UI bundle, and installed desktops pick it up without a new Electron build.

**Prior art check — `streamer.share` does _not_ do this.** Its packaged app loads the renderer from a local file inside the asar (`loadFile(join(mainDir, '../renderer/index.html'))`, `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1485`). What it has is `electron-updater` against a Cloudflare R2 feed — full signed app replacement, not UI-only. There is no remote bundle, no `app://` handler, no manifest fetch, and none of the hardening (no CSP, no `will-navigate` guard, `sandbox: false`) that remote UI would need. There is nothing to copy from there.

**Agendex, however, is already most of the way there.** The packaged app does not use `file://` at all — it boots an in-process Hono server and serves the SPA over `http://localhost:<ephemeral-port>` from a plain directory on disk:

```ts
// packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:65-72
function resolveClientDistDir(): string {
  if (app.isPackaged) return join(process.resourcesPath, 'client'); // extraResources, outside the asar
  return join(__dirname, '../../../ee/dist');
}
```

So the whole mechanism reduces to **pointing that resolver at a downloaded, verified bundle under `userData`**. The origin never changes, which means the preload bridge, the relative `/api/v1` fetches (`packages/web/src/client/lib/api.ts:1`), the WebSocket URL, the `localStorage` bootstrap injection, and the `shouldOpenNavigationExternally` guard all keep working untouched.

**Outcome:** merge a UI change → publish a signed bundle from CI → installed desktops verify, stage, and hot-reload it. `resources/client` remains the immutable offline floor, so the app never depends on the network to start.

### Decisions taken

| Decision          | Choice                                                                              |
| ----------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Hosting / trigger | GitHub Releases on a fixed rolling tag, published by an explicit workflow           |
| Apply timing      | Stage in background, prompt "Reload now / Later"; applies on next launch either way |
| Trust             | Ed25519 detached signature over the manifest, public key baked into the main bundle |
| CSP               | Out of scope — separate follow-up                                                   |

### Explicitly rejected

Pointing the window straight at `https://app.agendex.dev`. It breaks the relative `/api/v1` and WS calls (they would hit the cloud host, not the local Hono server), trips `shouldOpenNavigationExternally` into `shell.openExternal`, kills offline start, and exposes the privileged `agendexDesktop` bridge to a remote origin. Serving downloaded bundles through the existing local server avoids all four.

---

## Design

**Bundle store** — `app.getPath('userData')/ui/`:

```
ui/
  current.json          { revision, dir, pendingVerify, quarantined: number[] }
  1753920000/           extracted packages/ee/dist
  1753833600/           previous revision (kept for one cycle)
  .staging-<rev>/       in-progress extraction
```

Never write into `resources/client` — on macOS it lives inside the signed, notarized `.app` and mutating it breaks the signature.

**Revision scheme.** `uiRevision` is the **git commit Unix timestamp** of the commit the bundle was built from — monotonic on a linear `main`, no counter to maintain, deterministic in CI. The shipped floor is stamped with the same value at desktop build time so both sides compare on equal terms. When git is unavailable (local dev), stamp `0`, which means any published bundle outranks it.

**Compatibility gate.** `minShellVersion` is a committed constant (`packages/ee/ui-bundle.config.json`), bumped by hand whenever the UI starts depending on a new IPC method or `/api/v1` route. A shell ignores any bundle whose `minShellVersion` exceeds `app.getVersion()`. This is what keeps UI and shell decoupled safely.

**The manifest is desired state, not "newer only."** Republishing it at a lower revision is the rollback path; `"pinToShipped": true` is the kill switch that discards staged bundles and reverts to `resources/client`. Both take effect on the next check.

**Crash safety.** Before the first load of a newly activated bundle, `current.json` records `pendingVerify: true`. The renderer calls `window.agendexDesktop.signalUiReady()` once React mounts. If that signal does not arrive within ~15s, or `did-fail-load` fires, the revision goes on the `quarantined` list and the shell reverts to the shipped floor and reloads. Only _downloaded_ bundles are sentinel-checked; the floor is never quarantined.

---

## Implementation

### 1. Make the static mount resolvable at request time

`packages/app/src/server/node-server.ts`

- Widen `StartNodeServerOptions.clientDistDir` to `string | (() => string)` (keep `string` working — `startNodeServer` is also exported for the OSS CLI).
- `mountStaticFromDir` resolves the root **per request** rather than closing over it, so swapping the active bundle takes effect on reload with no server restart. It already does per-request `fs` calls, so this costs nothing.
- Add cache headers, which do not exist today: `Cache-Control: no-store` on the `index.html` responses (both the direct hit and the SPA fallback), `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` for content-hashed files under `/assets/`. Without `no-store` on the HTML, a reload can serve a stale document referencing deleted asset hashes.
- Note for later: `MIME_TYPES` (line 24) doubles as the served-extension allowlist via `looksLikeAssetPath`. Any new asset type in a future bundle must be added here or it 404s.

### 2. Bundle store + verification

New directory `packages/desktop/src/main/ui-bundle/`:

- **`config.ts`** — feed URL `https://github.com/Tyru5/Agendex/releases/download/desktop-ui-channel/ui-manifest.json`, overridable via `AGENDEX_UI_FEED_URL`; disable switch `AGENDEX_UI_UPDATE_DISABLED`. Mirrors the `getSiteUrl` env-override pattern in `cloud-auth.ts:18-25`.
- **`keys.ts`** — the Ed25519 public key as a PEM constant.
- **`manifest.ts`** — fetch `ui-manifest.json` and `ui-manifest.json.sig` (send `Cache-Control: no-cache` and a `?t=` buster; GitHub caches release assets). Verify with Node's built-in `crypto.verify(null, rawBytes, publicKey, sig)` — Ed25519 needs `null` as the algorithm, and this adds **no new dependency**. The signature is **detached over the raw manifest bytes**, which sidesteps JSON canonicalization entirely. Parse only after the signature verifies.
- **`store.ts`** — read/write `current.json`, resolve the active dir, quarantine list, prune to current + previous.
- **`install.ts`** — stream the tarball to a temp file, hash it with `crypto.createHash('sha256')` and compare against the signed manifest, extract into `.staging-<rev>`, then activate by writing `current.json`. Extract to a fresh unique dir and only then flip the pointer — never `rename` over an existing directory (fails on Windows).

Add **`tar`** (node-tar, pure JS, has built-in path-traversal guards) to `packages/desktop` dependencies. `electron.vite.config.ts` has `externalizeDeps: false`, so it gets bundled into `out/main` with no packaging changes.

### 3. The UI updater

New `packages/desktop/src/main/ui-updater.ts`, modeled directly on `desktop-updater.ts` — same dependency-injected shape (`createUiUpdater({ fetchImpl, store, isPackaged, promptToReload, onStateChange, log, checkIntervalMs, setTimeoutFn, ... })`), same `UpdateState` status union, so it is unit-testable without Electron. Reuse the 4h interval / 10s initial delay defaults.

One deliberate difference from `desktop-updater.ts`: **do not gate on `isPortableWindowsBuild()`**. Windows portable builds cannot self-update the app, but `userData` is still writable, so they _can_ take UI updates. That is a real win for portable users.

### 4. Wire into the main process

`packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts`

- `resolveClientDistDir()` becomes: packaged → active bundle from the store if it passes the `minShellVersion` gate, is not quarantined, and out-ranks the shipped floor; otherwise `join(process.resourcesPath, 'client')`.
- Pass the resolver (not a string) into `startNodeServer`.
- Start the updater alongside the existing one; register IPC `agendex:ui-update:check` / `:apply` / `:get-state` in `desktop-ipc.ts`, push state via `mainWindow.webContents.send('agendex:ui-update:state', …)`, mirroring `agendex:update:*`.
- Prompt with `dialog.showMessageBox`, matching the existing `promptToRestart` flow. Apply = flip the pointer, then `mainWindow.webContents.reloadIgnoringCache()`.
- Add `agendex:ui-ready` for the boot sentinel, and extend the `did-fail-load` path in `window-loader.ts` to trigger quarantine + revert.

### 5. Preload + renderer surface

- `packages/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` — extend the `agendexDesktop` bridge with `checkForUiUpdates`, `applyUiUpdate`, `getUiUpdateState`, `getUiRevision`, `signalUiReady`. Re-dispatch `agendex:ui-update:state` as a `CustomEvent`, exactly as the existing `agendex:update:state` handler does (lines 56-58).
- `packages/ee/src/hooks/useDesktopUiUpdate.ts` — new hook alongside `useDesktopUpdate.ts`, same listener pattern.
- `packages/ee/src/main.tsx` — call `window.agendexDesktop?.signalUiReady?.()` after React mounts. This is what proves the _bundle's_ JS actually ran; the preload firing only proves the shell loaded.
- `packages/ee/src/components/SettingsPage.tsx` — show the UI revision next to the existing app version and update controls.

### 6. Build + publish

- **`scripts/build-ui-bundle.mjs`** (new) — reads `packages/ee/ui-bundle.config.json` for `minShellVersion`, derives `uiRevision` from `git log -1 --format=%ct`, writes `packages/ee/dist/ui-bundle.json`, tars `packages/ee/dist` to a temp path, gzips, hashes, and emits `ui-manifest.json`. Signing happens only in CI, from `UI_BUNDLE_SIGNING_KEY`.
- **`packages/desktop/package.json`** — `build` script also runs the stamping half of that script, so `resources/client` carries a real `ui-bundle.json` and the floor comparison is meaningful.
- **`.github/workflows/ui-release.yml`** (new) — `workflow_dispatch` + `push: tags: ui-v*`. Build `packages/ee`, produce the bundle, sign the manifest, then `gh release upload --clobber` all three assets onto the fixed `desktop-ui-channel` tag. Reuses the existing `GITHUB_TOKEN`; no new credentials, no new infra.

Generate the key pair once with `openssl genpkey -algorithm ed25519`; private key → repo secret `UI_BUNDLE_SIGNING_KEY`, public key → `keys.ts`.

---

## Critical files

| File                                                              | Change                                          |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| `packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:65-110`                       | `resolveClientDistDir` → resolver; wire updater |
| `packages/app/src/server/node-server.ts:93-134`                   | per-request root resolution + cache headers     |
| `packages/desktop/src/main/ui-bundle/*`                           | **new** — manifest, verify, install, store      |
| `packages/desktop/src/main/ui-updater.ts`                         | **new** — clone of `desktop-updater.ts` shape   |
| `packages/desktop/src/preload/index.ts:123-193`                   | extend the bridge                               |
| `packages/ee/src/main.tsx`                                        | `signalUiReady()` after mount                   |
| `scripts/build-ui-bundle.mjs`, `.github/workflows/ui-release.yml` | **new**                                         |

Reuse rather than rewrite: `createDesktopUpdater` (`desktop-updater.ts`) as the structural template, `loadWithRetry` (`window-loader.ts`) for the reload path, the `loadModePref`/`saveModePref` JSON-in-`userData` pattern (`dashboard-mode.ts`) for `current.json`, and the `agendex:update:state` CustomEvent bridge as the model for the new state channel.

Not a concern, worth knowing: the UI bundle bakes `VITE_*` env at build time, but `packages/ee/src/lib/convex-client.ts` deliberately derives the Convex URL at runtime for desktop ("the desktop build needs no baked Convex env"). A remotely-fetched bundle is therefore safe on that axis.

---

## Verification

**Unit** — `bun test packages/desktop`, following the DI style already used for the updater. Cover: revision comparison and the floor tie-break; `minShellVersion` rejection; bad signature rejected; SHA-256 mismatch rejected; tar path-traversal entry rejected; quarantine on missing `ui-ready`; downgrade and `pinToShipped` kill switch; prune keeps exactly two. Add the new test files to the `Run desktop-related tests` step in `desktop-release.yml:119`.

**Local end-to-end**, without touching production:

1. `bun run desktop:build && bun run --cwd packages/desktop dist` → install from `packages/desktop/release/`.
2. `node scripts/build-ui-bundle.mjs` against a locally-signed test key; serve the three assets from any static server.
3. Launch with `AGENDEX_UI_FEED_URL=http://localhost:8080/ui-manifest.json` and `AGENDEX_DESKTOP_QA_USER_DATA_DIR` pointed at a scratch dir.
4. Confirm: dialog appears → reload shows the new UI → `userData/ui/<rev>/` populated and `current.json` correct.
5. Restart and confirm it comes back on the downloaded bundle, not the floor.

**Negative paths, all of which must leave a working app:**

- Corrupt one byte of the tarball → rejected on checksum, floor still serves.
- Re-sign the manifest with a wrong key → rejected, floor still serves.
- Publish a bundle whose `main.tsx` throws immediately → sentinel times out, revision quarantined, app reverts and reloads onto the floor.
- Take the feed offline entirely and cold-start → app starts normally on the last good bundle.
- Set `minShellVersion` above the installed version → bundle ignored, no download.

**Regression surface to re-check on the packaged build:** local API auth (`localStorage.agendex_token` injection), the WebSocket at `/api/v1/ws`, `agendex://` OAuth deep-link login, and that external links still leave via `shell.openExternal`. All four depend on the origin, which this change deliberately does not touch — verifying them confirms that.

## Follow-ups (not in scope)

- CSP via `onHeadersReceived` (`script-src 'self'`, plus `style-src`/`font-src` for the cross-origin Google Fonts load). Worth doing now that remote code executes, but it needs its own verification pass against Tailwind/React inline styles.
- Differential updates — only fetch changed content-hashed assets. Premature at ~2.5 MB gzipped.

---

That's the whole file, verbatim. Still nothing implemented — approve on your machine when you're back, or tell me here what you'd change and I'll revise the plan file.

Implemented and verified. Nothing committed — the working tree is staged for your review.

## What it does

The packaged app already served its UI over `http://localhost:<port>` from a directory on disk, so the core change is that `packages/app/src/server/node-server.ts` now resolves that directory **per request** instead of capturing it once. `resolveShippedClientDistDir()` stays the offline floor; a verified bundle in `userData/ui/<revision>/` takes precedence. Origin never changes, so the preload bridge, relative `/api/v1` calls, the WebSocket, and the external-navigation guard were untouched.

## Verification

| Check                                  | Result               |
| -------------------------------------- | -------------------- |
| New tests (`ui-bundle` + `ui-updater`) | **56 pass**          |
| `packages/desktop` + `packages/app`    | **159 pass, 0 fail** |
| `bun run check`                        | **0 errors**         |
| `tsc --noEmit` on `packages/desktop`   | **clean**            |
| Real end-to-end packaging + install    | **passed**           |

That last one is worth calling out: I built the real EE client, packaged it (3.10 MB gzipped), signed it with a throwaway Ed25519 key, served it over local HTTP, and drove the actual `fetchSignedManifest` → `installBundle` → store path. It verified the signature, hash-checked and extracted the tarball, resolved from shipped → downloaded bundle, and **rejected the same feed when re-run with a wrong public key**. Temp artifacts cleaned up; `packages/ee/ui-release` added to `.gitignore`.

## Three things you should know

**The feature is inert until you provision the key.** `BAKED_PUBLIC_KEY_PEM` in `ui-bundle/keys.ts` is an empty string. Verification fails closed on an empty key, so shells keep serving their shipped UI rather than trusting the feed — safe by default, but nothing updates until you generate the pair, paste the public half in, and add `UI_BUNDLE_SIGNING_KEY` as a repo secret. As agreed, I didn't touch the private key.

**I reformatted 3 files I didn't otherwise touch.** `.amp/plugins/{fable,grok-45,kimi-k3}-mode.ts` came in with commit `239d335` unformatted and already break `bun run check` on `main`. My new workflow runs that same gate, so it would have failed on its first run. Whitespace only — revert if you'd rather fix it separately.

**6 tests fail in the full suite, and they were already failing.** They're in `packages/cli`/`packages/shared` (sync payloads, tilde expansion, Kiro/Plannotator/OpenCode adapters). I confirmed this by running a pristine `git worktree` at `HEAD` and getting the identical 6 failures. Also pre-existing: type errors in `packages/web/.../PlanViewer.tsx`. Neither is mine, and I left both alone.

One deviation from the plan, in your favour: the UI updater does **not** disable itself for Windows portable builds the way the app updater does. Those can't self-replace, but `userData` is writable — so portable users get UI updates.

Root cause confirmed: Bun's `Database.close()` doesn't finalize outstanding prepared statements, so the connection stays open and Windows locks the file. `close(true)` even reports "database is locked". The production adapter uses `db.query()` (which `close()` handles), so this is test-only.

**576 pass, 0 fail.** `bun run check` clean.

All 6 were Windows-only — CI runs the full suite on `ubuntu-latest`, so they've been green there while failing on your machine. Two turned out to be real product bugs, not just brittle tests.

## Real bugs fixed

**Plan titles were full file paths on Windows** — `plan-service.ts:224` derived titles with `filePath.split('/').pop()`. On Windows the path is backslash-separated, so the split returned the whole string and every plan without an `# H1` heading was titled `C:\Users\...\code-only` instead of `code-only`. That's user-visible in the app, the CLI, and anything synced to the cloud. Now `basename(filePath, '.md')`, which also fixes `.replace('.md', '')` matching the first occurrence anywhere in a name rather than the extension.

**`~\` paths weren't accepted on Windows** — neither cmd nor PowerShell expands `~` in arguments passed to an exe, so `agendex upload ~\plans\x.md` reached the CLI with a literal tilde and fell through to relative-path resolution. Added `isHomeRelativePath()` in `config.ts`, used by both `expandHomePath` and `resolveUploadFilePath`, with the backslash form gated to Windows since a backslash is a legal POSIX filename character. Covered by a new test.

## Test-only fixes

- **`payload.test.ts`** — asserted against `/tmp/abs/plan.md` while `fileToSyncPayload` correctly `resolve()`s it (→ `C:\tmp\abs\plan.md`). Now resolves the expectation too.
- **`file-artifact-adapters.test.ts` / `plannotator.test.ts`** — compared native backslash paths against `/`-style suffixes. Normalized before asserting. Worth noting these two are correct to differ: adapters deliberately emit POSIX-normalized _workspaces_ for cross-platform identity keys, while `filePath` stays native because it gets opened and written back.
- **`structured-session.test.ts`** — EBUSY on teardown. I first assumed lazy Windows handle release and added retries; that didn't fix it, so I probed Bun directly. Actual cause: **`Database.close()` doesn't finalize outstanding prepared statements**, leaving the connection open and the file locked — `close(true)` reports it as "database is locked". Three statements in the test were never finalized. The production adapter uses `db.query()` and closes properly, so no product bug there. I corrected my initial comment and kept a smaller retry count as genuine Windows hygiene.

## Verification

Baseline-checked with a `git worktree` at HEAD: the same 8 `packages/shared` and 1 `packages/cli` tsc errors exist there unchanged (bun-types matcher mismatches, `WriteStream.fd`). My changes added none — the one `config.test.ts` line that moved shifted 81→82, exactly matching the import I added.

Still uncommitted, and the three `.amp/plugins` formatting fixes from earlier are still in the diff.

## User — 2026-08-01 21:33

What's the command you recommend to generate the signing key?

## Assistant — 2026-08-01 21:34

Not openssl — it isn't on your PATH (I hit that earlier in this session when I tried it for the end-to-end test). Node is, and it produces the identical key type, so I'd use that.

**Generate it outside the repo** — `*.pem` isn't in `.gitignore`:

```powershell
cd $env:TEMP

# Writes the private key, prints the matching public key to paste into keys.ts
node -e "const{generateKeyPairSync}=require('node:crypto');const{privateKey,publicKey}=generateKeyPairSync('ed25519');require('node:fs').writeFileSync('ui-signing-key.pem',privateKey.export({type:'pkcs8',format:'pem'}));process.stdout.write(publicKey.export({type:'spki',format:'pem'}))"
```

Then store the private half and destroy the local copy:

```powershell
gh secret set UI_BUNDLE_SIGNING_KEY --repo Tyru5/Agendex --body (Get-Content -Raw ui-signing-key.pem)
Remove-Item ui-signing-key.pem
```

Two PowerShell-specific notes: `gh secret set NAME < file` won't work here — PowerShell has no `<` input redirection — hence `--body (Get-Content -Raw ...)`, which also avoids the line-by-line mangling you'd get from piping `Get-Content` without `-Raw`.

Paste the printed `-----BEGIN PUBLIC KEY-----` block into `BAKED_PUBLIC_KEY_PEM` in `packages/desktop/src/main/ui-bundle/keys.ts`.

**Confirming they match:** the first `ui-release` run's "Verify the signature" step signs, verifies, and prints the public key derived from the secret, with a reminder that it must equal `keys.ts`. So if the paste goes wrong you'll see it in CI logs rather than as silently-rejected updates on user machines.

If you'd rather do this on a Mac, `openssl genpkey -algorithm ed25519` + `openssl pkey -pubout` gives byte-identical output.

One thing worth fixing: the comment block in `keys.ts` currently documents only the openssl route, which won't run on your main machine. Want me to rewrite it with the Node commands above?

Confirmed — with one important nuance. The feature splits into two halves, and they have **different** pipeline status.

## The stamping half _is_ wired in ✅

`packages/desktop/package.json` → `"build"` chains `node ../../scripts/build-ui-bundle.mjs --stamp-only`. Both `desktop-build.yml` (lines 74, 129) and `desktop-release.yml` (lines 144, 296) run `bun run desktop:build`, so every desktop build — CI and real release — stamps `resources/client`. Already proven in the merge run:

```
ui-bundle: revision 1785624593 2026-08-01 (a170453) (needs shell >= 1.4.15)
```

## The publishing half is _not_ wired in ❌

`ui-release.yml` triggers only on:

```yaml
on:
  workflow_dispatch: # manual
  push:
    tags: ['ui-v*']
```

Nothing else reaches it. Grepping every workflow plus both `package.json`s, `release:ui:bundle` appears **only inside `ui-release.yml` itself** — no other workflow calls it, and `desktop-release.yml` doesn't chain to it. Supporting evidence: zero `ui-v*` tags on origin, zero runs of the workflow ever, no `desktop-ui-channel` release. Local files are byte-identical to `main`.

## So, concretely

Merging to `main` publishes nothing. Cutting a `desktop-v*` release publishes nothing either — it only bakes a newer shipped floor into the installers. A UI bundle reaches users **only** when you manually dispatch `ui-release.yml` or push a `ui-v*` tag.

That is what you chose — deliberate publishing over auto-on-merge, because you wanted rollout to stay an explicit act with a rollback path. But it's worth being clear that "merged and CI green" and "users get UI updates" are entirely disconnected right now, and will stay that way until the first dispatch.

One genuine gap in that design, since you're looking at it: `desktop-release.yml` and `ui-release.yml` are independent, so it's possible to ship a desktop release whose shipped floor is _newer_ than the published channel manifest. That's handled safely at runtime — `store.ts` has the floor win ties, so users just keep the newer shipped UI rather than regressing — but the channel would then be serving a stale manifest that no one takes, which is confusing to debug later. Worth deciding whether a desktop release should also refresh the channel. No changes made; happy to plan that if you want it.

Correct. `Release Desktop UI` is registered and **active** (id `325288981`), and your token has `repo`, which is what the workflow-dispatch API needs — the `workflow` scope is only required to _modify_ workflow files, not to trigger them.

**Normal publish:**

```bash
gh workflow run ui-release.yml --repo Tyru5/Agendex
```

Defaults to `main`. Add `--ref <branch>` to publish from elsewhere.

**Or via the tag trigger:**

```bash
git tag ui-v1 && git push origin ui-v1
```

**Kill switch:**

```bash
gh workflow run ui-release.yml --repo Tyru5/Agendex -f pin_to_shipped=true
```

Two things worth knowing before you rely on these.

**The kill-switch flag is the one path I haven't verified, and it fails in the wrong direction.** The workflow gates on `${{ inputs.pin_to_shipped == true }}` / `!= true`. `gh workflow run -f` sends inputs as JSON _strings_; GitHub is supposed to coerce them to the declared `type: boolean` in the `inputs` context, but this is a long-standing Actions sharp edge and I wrote that condition without being able to test it.

The asymmetry matters: the no-input path is safe either way — the default `false` correctly builds and publishes. But if coercion misbehaves on the kill switch, `== true` goes false _and_ `!= true` goes true, so instead of pinning users back to their shipped UI you'd **publish a fresh bundle** — the opposite of what you want, at the exact moment you least want it. Making the conditions string-tolerant (`== 'true'` or `fromJSON`) is a one-line change that removes the ambiguity. I'd suggest doing that before you ever need it in anger; say the word and I'll make it.

**Dispatch is idempotent per commit.** The revision is the checked-out commit's timestamp, so re-running on the same `main` HEAD regenerates an identical bundle and clobbers the channel assets with the same content. Harmless — just don't expect a second dispatch to produce a "newer" revision without a new commit.

```
      staged = null;
      setState({ status: 'no-update' });
      if (wasOverridden) applyReload();
      return;
    }

=== store.ts: revertToShipped ===
    revertToShipped() {
      const state = readState();
      if (state.revision === null && !state.pendingVerify) return;
      writeState({ revision: null, pendingVerify: false, quarantined: state.quarantined });
… 8 more lines
```

</details>

The name is doing a lot of work there — let me unpack it.

## What it is

A signed manifest that carries no bundle, just `pinToShipped: true`. When a desktop fetches it, it discards whatever downloaded UI it's running and falls back to the UI baked into its installed app — `resources/client`, the copy that came with the `.exe`/`.dmg`.

It's the "everybody stop, go back to what you had" lever.

## The gap it covers

You already have automatic protection, but only against _hard_ failures:

- Renderer doesn't signal ready within 20s → quarantine that revision, revert, reload
- `did-fail-load` / `render-process-gone` → same

Those catch a white screen or a bundle that throws on load. They do **not** catch a bundle that boots perfectly and is wrong — a broken save button, a mislabelled column, a data-corrupting regression. That bundle renders, signals ready, and every client happily keeps serving it forever. Nothing automatic will ever pull it back.

That's the case the kill switch exists for.

## Mechanically

```ts
if (manifest.pinToShipped) {
  const wasOverridden = store.readState().revision !== null;
  store.revertToShipped(); // active revision -> null
  store.prune();
  if (wasOverridden) applyReload();
}
```

`revertToShipped()` sets the active revision to `null`, which makes `resolveActiveDir()` return the shipped directory. The reload is unconditional — no "Reload now / Later" prompt — because you're moving users _onto_ known-good code, so waiting for consent would just prolong the outage.

Note it leaves the `quarantined` list untouched: pinning isn't a permanent blocklist, it's a state you can lift later by publishing a normal manifest again.

## How fast it reaches people

It's a pull, not a push. Clients check on their 4-hour background timer, or immediately if someone hits **Check for Interface Updates** in Settings. So worst case is ~4 hours for a user sitting in the app. There's no way to force it faster — that's inherent to the design you picked.

## Kill switch vs. rollback

Two different tools, and rollback is usually the better one:

|                                                   | Reverts to                                    | Use when                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Rollback** — republish manifest at revision N-1 | The previous _bundle_                         | You know which revision was good                              |
| **Kill switch** — `pin_to_shipped=true`           | Whatever shipped in each user's installed app | You don't know what's good, or every recent bundle is suspect |

Rollback is more precise. The kill switch is blunter: users land on the UI from their installed app version, which differs per user depending on how stale their desktop install is — someone on an old `desktop-v1.4.x` gets an old UI. But it always works, requires no judgement about which revision to trust, and needs no bundle to point at.

## One property worth appreciating

The pinned manifest is still Ed25519-signed. So nobody can pin your users without the repo secret — the kill switch isn't a denial-of-service vector someone else can pull on you.

And this is exactly the path whose `-f pin_to_shipped=true` boolean coercion I flagged as untested — the failure mode being that it publishes a real bundle instead of pinning, precisely when you're trying to stop the bleeding.

Two separate problems, and the first one masked the second.

## Why nothing fired

**The tag was never created.** No `ui-v*` ref exists locally or on origin, and `git rev-parse --verify ui-v1` fails. Since `git tag X && git push origin X` short-circuits, a failed `git tag` means the push never ran — and you'd only have seen the tag error, which is easy to miss scrolling past. Most likely you were in **PowerShell 5.1**, where `&&` is a parse error and neither command executes. It works fine in PS7 or Git Bash.

## The problem that would have bitten you next

You're on `feature/desktop-ui-updates` at `aa835ae`, **8 commits behind main**. Tagging `HEAD` would have published a bundle built from that commit:

```
bundle from aa835ae : 1785620536   ← below the floor
shipped floor       : 1785624593
bundle from main    : 1785724363   ← above the floor
```

The revision is the commit timestamp, and `store.ts` has the shipped floor win ties:

```ts
if (stamp.revision <= shippedStamp().revision) return shippedDir;
```

So a bundle from `aa835ae` would have been **silently ignored by every client**. Green workflow, assets uploaded, manifest published, zero effect — and nothing in the logs would tell you why. That's a nasty failure mode to debug.

Tagging `main` avoids it: `1785724363` clears the floor.

## What to run

From **Git Bash** (or PS7), tagging `main` explicitly rather than whatever your branch happens to be on:

```bash
git fetch origin
git tag ui-v1 origin/main
git push origin ui-v1
```

That also picks up the three UI-affecting commits you're missing — `persist empty-state plan view`, `default empty state to list view`, and `unify trivia timeout handling` — so the first published bundle reflects current `main` rather than a two-hour-old snapshot.

`ui-release.yml` is confirmed present on `main`, and GitHub has it registered as `Release Desktop UI` (id `325288981`, active), so the tag trigger will resolve.

Two notes for the run itself: it publishes from whatever commit the tag points at, so verify the "Verify the signature" step prints a public key matching `keys.ts` — that's your first real end-to-end confirmation the secret and the baked key are a pair. And if you ever want to re-publish, you'll need a _new_ tag name; re-pushing `ui-v1` won't re-trigger.

Plan written to `C:\Users\Tyrus\.claude\plans\create-a-plan-that-drifting-giraffe.md`.

The key finding from the research: an agent **cannot** reliably reconstruct a conversation from its own context, because context gets summarized as a session grows — "the entire conversation" is exactly what it can no longer see. So the skill has to read the session JSONL that Claude Code writes to `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl`.

I verified the format against this session rather than assuming it. The one thing most likely to be gotten wrong: **a `user` line is only a human prompt when it has no `toolUseResult`** — 233 of the 99+233 user lines here are tool results wearing the user role. Miss that and the export is noise. Also confirmed `message.content` is sometimes a plain string rather than a block array, and that `tool_use.id` ↔ `tool_result.tool_use_id` is the join that pairs calls with output.

Shape: `SKILL.md` plus a dependency-free Node script under `~/.claude/skills/export-conversation/`. The parsing is fiddly enough that bundling a script is much more reliable than each agent hand-rolling `jq` — and skills here already support bundled files.

Your three choices are baked in. On the output location: I've kept your cwd choice and added a stderr warning when the file lands in a git working tree without being ignored — visible risk, unchanged behaviour. Exports routinely contain file contents and command output, so it's worth seeing before it gets staged.

Approve in Helium, or tell me to switch to build mode and I'll implement it.

```
    if (error?.code === 'EPIPE') process.exit(0);

… 3 more lines
```

Done. The skill is installed globally at `~/.claude/skills/export-conversation/` — `SKILL.md` plus a dependency-free Node script — so every agent in every project can use it.

```bash
node ~/.claude/skills/export-conversation/export-conversation.mjs
```

## Verified

| Check                                | Result                                                         |
| ------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Default export                       | 23 user / 22 assistant turns, 268 tool calls, 335 KB           |
| `--no-tools`                         | 93 KB — tool sections gone                                     |
| `--full`                             | 676 KB — no truncation                                         |
| `--thinking`                         | 18/18 reasoning blocks rendered on a session that records them |
| `--stdout`                           | writes no file; pipes to `head` cleanly                        |
| `--session` / `--list` / `--project` | resolve correctly, incl. from a different project              |
| Git warning                          | fires in-repo, silent outside                                  |
| Cleanup                              | no artifacts left; `git status` clean of exports               |

## Three things testing changed

**Turn merging.** The first run produced 367 assistant headings for 23 prompts — Claude Code writes each streamed block group as its own JSONL line. Consecutive assistant entries now merge into one turn, which is what a reader means by "a turn". 367 → 22.

**`--thinking` looked broken.** It produced a byte-identical file. Investigating across all local transcripts: **441 empty vs 176 populated** reasoning blocks — Claude Code persists reasoning text for some sessions and only a signature for others. This session is signature-only, so the flag was correct to add nothing. Rather than leave that baffling, it now prints a stderr note and the header reads `148, none recorded in this transcript`. Confirmed the flag does render real reasoning on a session that has it.

**An EPIPE crash.** `--stdout | head` threw an unhandled EPIPE. Piping to `head`/`less` is obvious usage, so that's now a clean exit.

## Worth knowing

The output is **~335 KB** for this conversation even in readable mode. That's five days and 268 tool calls, so it's inherent rather than fixable — `--no-tools` gets it to 93 KB if you want something closer to a chat log.

Counts drift by a few between consecutive runs because the transcript is appended to live, including by the export command itself. Expected, and noted in the skill docs.

The output location is your cwd as you chose. The git warning fires when it lands somewhere committable — worth heeding, since a 335 KB export of this session contains file contents, absolute paths, and command output.

# Export conversation to markdown

Renders a Claude Code session as a readable markdown document.

**Read the transcript from disk — do not reconstruct it from context.** Context
is summarised as a session grows, so by the time anyone asks for an export, the
earlier turns are exactly what the agent can no longer see. The complete record
lives in a JSONL file that this skill parses.

## Usage

```bash
node ~/.claude/skills/export-conversation/export-conversation.mjs
```

Writes `conversation-<session>-<date>.md` into the current directory and prints
where it went. No arguments needed for the common case.

## Flags

| Flag                  | Effect                                                                            |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `--full`              | No truncation — every tool input and result in full. Roughly 2× the default size. |
| `--thinking`          | Include assistant reasoning blocks (see caveat below).                            |
| `--no-tools`          | Prose only; drop tool calls entirely. Roughly ⅓ the default size.                 |
| `--sidechains`        | Include subagent turns, which are skipped by default.                             |
| `--out <path>`        | Write somewhere other than the working directory.                                 |
| `--stdout`            | Emit to stdout, touch no files. Safe to pipe to `head`/`less`.                    |
| `--session <id>`      | Export a specific session (prefix match is enough).                               |
| `--transcript <path>` | Point at a `.jsonl` directly, skipping all resolution.                            |
| `--project <path>`    | Resolve sessions for another directory instead of cwd.                            |
| `--list`              | List the sessions known for this project and exit.                                |

## Output shape

A metadata header (project, session id, time range, turn and tool counts, what
was included), then turns as `## User` / `## Assistant` with timestamps. Tool
calls render as collapsed `<details>` blocks, so the document reads as prose but
the evidence is one click away on GitHub and in most viewers.

In readable mode, tool inputs are reduced to their salient fields and results are
capped at ~20 lines with an explicit `… N more lines` marker — nothing is
silently dropped.

## Where it writes, and a caveat

Output lands in the **current working directory** by default. Exports routinely
contain file contents, absolute paths, and command output, so the script prints
a warning when it writes inside a git repository where the file is not ignored.
Delete it or gitignore it before committing. Use `--out` to write elsewhere.

## How it finds the transcript

Claude Code appends each session to:

```
~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl
```

The directory name is the project path with every `\`, `/`, `:` and `.` replaced
by `-`, e.g. `C:\Users\me\src\app` → `C--Users-me-src-app`. The newest `.jsonl`
in that directory is the session currently being written.

If resolution fails the script says where it looked and lists the project
directories it can see — usually the fix is `--project` or `--transcript`.

## Caveat: reasoning is not always recorded

Claude Code stores reasoning text for some sessions and only a cryptographic
signature for others. When a transcript has signature-only blocks, `--thinking`
correctly adds nothing; the script says so on stderr and the header reads
`N, none recorded in this transcript` rather than pretending the export is
incomplete. This is a property of the transcript, not a bug.

## Notes

- Requires only Node (no npm install). Streams the file, so multi-megabyte
  transcripts are fine.
- A `user` line in the JSONL is only a human prompt when it carries no tool
  result — in a busy session tool results outnumber real prompts ~10:1. The
  script already handles this; mention it if you are debugging the parser.
- The transcript is appended to live, so counts shift slightly between runs of
  the same session. That is expected.
